In September 2016, Amazon subsidiary [[Twitch (service)|Twitch]] announced features available to users with an Amazon Prime subscription (''Twitch Prime''), including monthly offers of video games and add-on content, and the ability to purchase a free subscription to a user's channel once per-month.<ref>{{Cite news|url=https://www.theverge.com/2018/8/20/17761760/twitch-prime-turbo-ad-free-benefit-end-over-september-14|title=Twitch Prime members will lose ad-free viewing next month|work=The Verge|access-date=August 20, 2018|archive-date=August 20, 2018|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180820213141/https://www.theverge.com/2018/8/20/17761760/twitch-prime-turbo-ad-free-benefit-end-over-september-14|url-status=live}}</ref><ref name="verge-twitchprime">{{cite web | url = https://www.theverge.com/2016/9/30/13125824/twitch-prime-amazon-ad-free-game-discounts | title = Twitch will be ad-free for all Amazon Prime subscribers | first = Nick | last = Statt | date = September 30, 2016 | access-date = September 30, 2016 | work = [[The Verge]] | archive-date = October 1, 2016 | archive-url = https://web.archive.org/web/20161001042339/https://www.theverge.com/2016/9/30/13125824/twitch-prime-amazon-ad-free-game-discounts | url-status = live }}</ref> Amazon then partnered with different game developers offering in-game loots as rewards to subscribers. Games included with the loot rewards were [[Apex Legends]], [[Legends of Runeterra]], [[Ultimate Team]], [[Teamfight Tactics]], [[Mobile Legends: Bang Bang]], [[Doom Eternal]], and more.<ref>{{Cite news|last=Cyre|first=Clayton|date=May 28, 2020|title=TWITCH PRIME GETTING MORE GAMES AND LOOT IN JUNE|work=COGconnected|url=https://cogconnected.com/2020/05/twitch-prime-games-loot-june/|url-status=live|archive-date=June 7, 2020|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200607135623/https://cogconnected.com/2020/05/twitch-prime-games-loot-june/}}</ref> In December 2016, Amazon began offering Prime membership for an alternative monthly, instead of yearly fee, of $10.99 per month,<ref>{{Cite news|url=https://money.cnn.com/2016/04/17/media/amazon-prime-video-new-pricing/index.html|title=Amazon Prime goes monthly in new challenge to Netflix|last=Stelter|first=Brian|work=CNNMoney|access-date=October 20, 2017|archive-date=October 20, 2017|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20171020135429/http://money.cnn.com/2016/04/17/media/amazon-prime-video-new-pricing/index.html|url-status=dead}}</ref> increased to $12.99 in February 2018.<ref>{{Cite news|url=https://www.cbsnews.com/news/amazon-boosts-monthly-prime-fee-by-almost-20-percent/|title=Amazon boosts monthly Prime fee by almost 20 percent|last=Fox Rubin|first=Ben|date=January 19, 2018|work=[[CNET]]|access-date=January 21, 2018|publisher=[[CBS Interactive]]|via=[[CBS News]]|archive-date=January 21, 2018|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180121075158/https://www.cbsnews.com/news/amazon-boosts-monthly-prime-fee-by-almost-20-percent/|url-status=live}}</ref> Amazon also announced Wickedly Prime, an own-brand line of food and beverages available to Prime members.<ref>{{cite web|url=https://techcrunch.com/2016/12/23/amazon-launches-its-newest-private-label-wickedly-prime/|title=Amazon launches its newest private label, Wickedly Prime|last=Perez|first=Sarah|website=TechCrunch|date=December 23, 2016 |access-date=December 23, 2016|archive-date=December 24, 2016|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20161224073252/https://techcrunch.com/2016/12/23/amazon-launches-its-newest-private-label-wickedly-prime/|url-status=live}}</ref>

Amazon announced Prime Wardrobe, a service that lets customers try on clothes before they pay, in June 2017.<ref>{{Cite news |last1=Wingfield |first1=Nick |title=Amazon Will Let Customers Try On Clothes Before Buying |work=[[The New York Times]] |date=June 20, 2017 |url=https://www.nytimes.com/2017/06/20/business/amazon-shopping-retail-fashion.html |issn=0362-4331  |access-date=June 21, 2017 |archive-date=June 21, 2017 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20170621173233/https://www.nytimes.com/2017/06/20/business/amazon-shopping-retail-fashion.html |url-status=live }}</ref> Also in 2017, Amazon announced the Prime Exclusive Phone program, which offers some [[smartphone]]s displaying Amazon ads on the [[lock screen]] from companies including [[LG Electronics|LG]], [[Motorola]] and [[Nokia]] at a discount.<ref>[https://www.theverge.com/circuitbreaker/2017/10/26/16552414/lg-amazon-prime-phone-discount-ads-g6 Amazon will now sell you high-end LG phones for less, if you'll put up with ads] {{Webarchive|url=https://web.archive.org/web/20171026160859/https://www.theverge.com/circuitbreaker/2017/10/26/16552414/lg-amazon-prime-phone-discount-ads-g6 |date=October 26, 2017 }} The Verge Retrieved October 26, 2017</ref> In January 2025, Amazon discontinued its Try Before You Buy service (formerly known as Prime Wardrobe).<ref>{{Cite web |last=Palmer |first=Annie |date=2025-01-10 |title=Amazon to shut down 'Try Before You Buy' rival to Stitch Fix |url=https://www.cnbc.com/2025/01/10/amazon-to-shut-down-stitch-fix-competitor-prime-try-before-you-buy.html |access-date=2026-10-11 |website=CNBC |language=en}}</ref>

In May 2018, Amazon increased the annual US Prime membership fee from $99 to $119.<ref>{{cite web|url=https://arstechnica.com/information-technology/2018/04/amazon-to-raise-annual-membership-price-of-prime-to-119/|title=Amazon will raise the price of Prime by $20 in a few weeks|date=April 27, 2018|access-date=April 27, 2018|archive-date=April 27, 2018|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180427154351/https://arstechnica.com/information-technology/2018/04/amazon-to-raise-annual-membership-price-of-prime-to-119/|url-status=live}}</ref> In June 2019, Amazon expanded its one-day delivery with Amazon Prime, stating that Prime Free One Day was available to U.S. members on more than 10 million products with no minimum purchase.<ref>{{cite web|url=https://blog.aboutamazon.com/amazon-prime/prime-free-one-day|title=Prime Free One Day|website=US Day One Blog|language=en|access-date=June 4, 2019|archive-date=June 3, 2019|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190603204619/https://blog.aboutamazon.com/amazon-prime/prime-free-one-day|url-status=live}}</ref>

In June 2018, Amazon launched Prime Australia, with a two-business-day delivery promise, free delivery of international orders, and Amazon Video.<ref name="amazonau.gcs-web.com" />

On March 3, 2020, Amazon announced it installed "mini-fulfillment centers" in select U.S. cities, including Dallas, Orlando, Philadelphia, and Phoenix to reduce same-day delivery times.<ref>{{cite web|url=https://techcrunch.com/2020/03/03/amazon-makes-its-same-day-delivery-service-faster-in-select-u-s-cities/|title=Amazon makes its same-day delivery service faster in select US cities|website=TechCrunch|date=March 3, 2020 |language=en-US|access-date=March 5, 2020|archive-date=November 13, 2020|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20201113192543/https://techcrunch.com/2020/03/03/amazon-makes-its-same-day-delivery-service-faster-in-select-u-s-cities/|url-status=live}}</ref> Later the same month, during the [[COVID-19 pandemic]], Prime express delivery dates for various in-stock items reached delays of up to a month in the US instead of the usual 1–2 days, as Amazon struggled to meet exceptional demand and announced it would prioritize the most essential items.<ref>{{cite web|url=https://www.vox.com/recode/2020/3/22/21190372/amazon-prime-delivery-delays-april-21-coronavirus-covid-19|title=Amazon Prime delivery delays are now as long as a month|last=Rey|first=Jason Del|date=March 22, 2020|website=Vox|language=en|access-date=March 25, 2020|archive-date=March 23, 2020|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200323021042/https://www.vox.com/recode/2020/3/22/21190372/amazon-prime-delivery-delays-april-21-coronavirus-covid-19|url-status=live}}</ref> By the end of 2020, Amazon Prime Pantry had been discontinued in all locations.<ref>{{Cite web|url=https://storebrands.com/amazon-ends-prime-pantry|title=Amazon ends Prime Pantry|work=Store Brands |access-date=February 15, 2023|archive-date=August 15, 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220815191711/https://storebrands.com/amazon-ends-prime-pantry|url-status=live}}</ref>

In February 2022, Amazon announced its first increase in almost four years for the annual US Prime membership fee from $119 to $139. The increase was due to higher labor and shipping costs.<ref>{{Cite news|title=Amazon raises prices for Prime members|newspaper=[[The Washington Post]]|url=https://www.washingtonpost.com/technology/2022/02/03/amazon-prime-price-increase/|access-date=February 15, 2023|archive-date=February 3, 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220203212913/https://www.washingtonpost.com/technology/2022/02/03/amazon-prime-price-increase/|url-status=live}}</ref>

In April 2022, Amazon launched "Buy with Prime", a new service for Prime members that allows them to purchase goods from partnered merchants and take advantage of Amazon's logistics, return and exchange services.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Thorbecke |first=Catherine |date=2022-04-21 |title=Amazon to let other online retailers offer Prime delivery service directly on their sites {{!}} CNN Business |url=https://www.cnn.com/2022/04/21/tech/amazon-buy-with-prime/index.html |access-date=2022-12-04 |website=CNN |language=en |archive-date=December 6, 2022 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20221206115821/https://www.cnn.com/2022/04/21/tech/amazon-buy-with-prime/index.html |url-status=live }}</ref>

On August 1, 2022, Amazon Prime was available for the first time in Indonesia, Thailand and the Philippines. Its presence aims to develop local and international content for users in Indonesia, the most considerable economic in Southeast Asia. Later, users can access Prime Video through the official website through various platforms, such as mobile devices, tablets, and laptops.<ref>{{cite news|title=Amazon Prime Video launches localized services for top three markets in Southeast Asia|url=https://techcrunch.com/2022/08/01/amazon-prime-video-launches-localized-services-for-top-three-markets-in-southeast-asia/|last=Forristal|first=Lauren|publisher=[[TechCrunch]]|date=August 1, 2022|access-date=December 3, 2022|archive-date=December 3, 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20221203072319/https://techcrunch.com/2022/08/01/amazon-prime-video-launches-localized-services-for-top-three-markets-in-southeast-asia/|url-status=live}}</ref>

=== 2023–present ===
In January 2023, Amazon announced the launch of RXPass, a prescription drug delivery service. It allows US Amazon Prime members to pay a $5 monthly fee for access to 60 medications. The service was launched immediately after the announcement except in states with specific prescription delivery requirements.  Beneficiaries of government healthcare programs such as Medicaid are not eligible for RxPass.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Meyersohn |first=Nathaniel |date=2023-01-24 |title=Amazon launches $5-a-month unlimited prescription plan {{!}} CNN Business |url=https://www.cnn.com/2023/01/24/business/amazon-prescription-drugs-rxpass/index.html |access-date=2023-01-26 |website=CNN |language=en |archive-date=February 9, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230209110020/https://www.cnn.com/2023/01/24/business/amazon-prescription-drugs-rxpass/index.html |url-status=live }}</ref> In addition, Prime Rx offers discounts on generic and brand-name medications and discounts at pharmacies including Amazon Pharmacy.<ref>{{cite news |last1=Landi |first1=Heather |title=Amazon Pharmacy expands RxPass subscription service to Medicare patients |url=https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/health-tech/amazon-pharmacy-expands-rxpass-subscription-service-medicare-patients |access-date=3 June 2026 |work=Fierce Healthcare |date=18 June 2024}}</ref>

In June 2023, the [[Federal Trade Commission|Federal Trade Commission (FTC)]] sued Amazon for its "years-long effort to enroll consumers into its Prime program without their consent while knowingly making it difficult for consumers to cancel their subscriptions".<ref>{{Cite web |last=Palmer |first=Annie |date=2023-06-21 |title=FTC sues Amazon over 'deceptive' Prime sign-up and cancellation process |url=https://www.cnbc.com/2023/06/21/ftc-sues-amazon-over-deceptive-prime-sign-up-and-cancellation-process.html |access-date=2023-06-29 |website=CNBC |language=en |archive-date=June 29, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230629222243/https://www.cnbc.com/2023/06/21/ftc-sues-amazon-over-deceptive-prime-sign-up-and-cancellation-process.html |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=2023-06-21 |title=FTC Takes Action Against Amazon for Enrolling Consumers in Amazon Prime Without Consent and Sabotaging Their Attempts to Cancel |url=https://www.ftc.gov/news-events/news/press-releases/2023/06/ftc-takes-action-against-amazon-enrolling-consumers-amazon-prime-without-consent-sabotaging-their |access-date=2023-06-29 |website=Federal Trade Commission |language=en |archive-date=June 29, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230629230957/https://www.ftc.gov/news-events/news/press-releases/2023/06/ftc-takes-action-against-amazon-enrolling-consumers-amazon-prime-without-consent-sabotaging-their |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=2021-01-14 |title=FTC Complaint: Ending an Amazon Prime Membership Is a Deceptive, Unlawful Ordeal |url=https://www.citizen.org/news/ftc-complaint-ending-an-amazon-prime-membership-is-a-deceptive-unlawful-ordeal/ |access-date=2023-06-29 |website=Public Citizen |language=en |archive-date=June 29, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230629222244/https://www.citizen.org/news/ftc-complaint-ending-an-amazon-prime-membership-is-a-deceptive-unlawful-ordeal/ |url-status=live }}</ref>

In April 2024, Amazon announced the launch of a new delivery service for groceries for Prime customers and those receiving government assistance for food. Regular Prime subscribers would be charged $9.99 per month on orders $35 and higher from [[Whole Foods Market|Whole Foods]] and [[Amazon Fresh]], along with other prominent chains like [[Save Mart Supermarkets|Save Mart]], [[Bartell Drugs]], [[Rite Aid]], and [[Pet Food Express]] and local grocery and specialty retail stores on the platform. Individuals on government assistance would be charged a reduced fee of $4.99 and would not need to be a Prime member. Amazon said it would be available in more than 3,500 locations around the country when announcing. Prior to the announcement, it was given a successful trial run in several cities in the US. It came at a time when competitors like [[Target Corporation|Target]] and [[Walmart]] were expanding their fresh food operations.<ref>{{Cite web |date=2024-04-23 |title=Amazon launches low-cost grocery delivery subscription plan in US |url=https://finance.yahoo.com/news/amazon-launches-low-cost-grocery-100312048.html |access-date=2024-04-23 |website=Yahoo Finance |language=en-US}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Mehta |first=Ivan |date=2024-04-23 |title=Amazon launches a new grocery delivery subscription in the US |url=https://techcrunch.com/2024/04/23/amazon-launches-a-new-grocery-delivery-subscription-in-the-u-s/ |access-date=2024-04-23 |website=TechCrunch |language=en-US}}</ref>

In the United States and in the United Kingdom, Amazon Prime has an Amazon Household program where members of a household can share Amazon Prime delivery benefits and access to Prime Video. Amazon rebranded Amazon Household to Amazon Family in 2025.<ref>{{Cite web |date=2023-06-16 |title=How to use Amazon Household to share Prime benefits with your whole family |url=https://www.aboutamazon.co.uk/news/amazon-prime/amazon-household-prime-benefits |access-date=2024-12-22 |website=UK About Amazon |language=en}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Chen |first=Connie |date=2023-03-14 |title=How to use Amazon Household to share Prime benefits with your whole family |url=https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/amazon-prime/how-does-amazon-household-work |access-date=2024-12-22 |website=www.aboutamazon.com |language=en}}</ref> In other countries such as Germany, users can only share delivery benefits.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Share Your Amazon Prime Benefits - Amazon Customer Service |url=https://www.amazon.de/gp/help/customer/display.html?nodeId=GWZ7QXD2X8WL8YE8 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20241209131748/https://www.amazon.de/gp/help/customer/display.html?nodeId=GWZ7QXD2X8WL8YE8 |archive-date=December 9, 2024 |access-date=2024-12-22 |website=www.amazon.de |language=en-gb |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=2024-09-12 |title=Wie kannst du Prime-Vorteile teilen? |url=https://www.aboutamazon.de/news/amazon-prime-und-shopping/amazon-prime-vorteile-teilen |access-date=2024-12-22 |website=DE About Amazon |language=de}}</ref>

In October 2024, Amazon announced a fuel discount program allowing Prime members to save 10 cents per gallon at around 7,000 participating BP, Amoco, and ampm stations across the United States. In 2025, to coincide with Prime Day, Amazon expanded the program to more than 7,500 participating BP, Amoco, and ampm stations. The deal was launched to compete with a similar fuel savings program with Walmart+ for ExxonMobil stations.<ref>{{cite web | title=How Amazon Prime can get you $1 off each gallon of gas on your July 4 weekend travels | date=July 3, 2025 | url=https://www.wcpo.com/amazon-prime-gas-discount-how-you-can-save-on-gas-at-amoco-and-bp-gas-stations }}</ref>

[[Amazon Pharmacy]] launched in November 2020. It sells medications at what Amazon claims are “affordable prices to customers in the United States, with or without insurance". Amazon Pharmacy discloses pricing with Prime and insurance directly on the Amazon site.<ref>{{cite web |last1=Chen |first1=Connie |title=Amazon's full-service online pharmacy makes it easy and affordable to shop for medication. Here's how it works. |url=https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/retail/how-does-amazon-pharmacy-work |website=aboutamazon.com |publisher=[[Amazon (company)|Amazon]] |access-date=6 May 2026}}</ref> Amazon Prime membership is integrated with Amazon Pharmacy and allows customers in the United States to order prescription medications for home delivery with pharmacist support. While a Prime membership is not required to use the pharmacy, members may receive benefits such as prescription delivery and access to certain medication discounts. The service accepts insurance plans and prescriptions can be sent by healthcare providers.<ref>{{cite web |last1=Chen |first1=Connie |title=Amazon's full-service online pharmacy makes it easy and affordable to shop for medication. Here's how it works.|url=https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/retail/how-does-amazon-pharmacy-work|website=aboutamazon.com |publisher=[[Amazon (company)|Amazon]] |access-date=6 May 2026}}</ref><ref>{{cite news |last1= |first1= |title=Amazon makes Alexa+ AI assistant available to everyone in the U.S. nearly a year after launch |url=https://www.reuters.com/business/amazon-launches-online-pharmacy-new-contest-with-drug-retail-2020-11-17/ |access-date=6 May 2026 |work=[[Reuters]] |date=November 17, 2020}}</ref><ref>{{cite news |last1=González |first1=Oriana |title=Amazon launches online pharmacy, prescription delivery service |url=https://www.axios.com/2020/11/17/amazon-online-pharmacy-launch-coronavirus |access-date=6 May 2026 |work=[[Axios (website)|Axios]] |date=November 17, 2020}}</ref>

Since 2020, Prime members have access to Amazon Kids+, which, according to Amazon, has “ad-free and age-appropriate books, games, videos, music, apps, and Alexa Skills".<ref>{{cite web |title=Introducing Amazon Kids and Amazon Kids+ |url=https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/devices/introducing-amazon-kids-and-amazon-kids |website=amazon.com |publisher=[[Amazon (company)|Amazon]] |access-date=3 June 2026}}</ref>

After December 31, 2023, customers could no longer access files through the Amazon Drive apps and instead were directed to the Amazon Photos website. Prime members receive unlimited full-resolution photo storage and 5 GB of video storage. Customers can also share photos with up to five family members and display images on.<ref>{{cite news |last1=Humphries |first1=Matthew |title=Amazon Drive Shuts Down Dec. 31, 2023 |url=https://www.pcmag.com/news/amazon-drive-shuts-down-dec-31-2023 |access-date=3 June 2026 |work=[[PCMag]] |date=29 July 2022}}</ref>

In February 2026, Amazon launched its Alexa+ AI assistant in the United States. The service was priced at $19.99 per month, with access included for Amazon Prime members.<ref>{{cite news |last1=Palmer |first1=Annie |title=Amazon makes Alexa+ AI assistant available to everyone in the U.S. nearly a year after launch |url=https://www.cnbc.com/2026/02/04/amazon-alexa-plus-us-releas.html |access-date=6 May 2026 |work=[[CNBC]] |date=4 February 2026}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |last1=Rausch |first1=Daniel |title=Alexa+ now available to everyone in the US—and free for Prime members |url=https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/devices/alexa-plus-available-free-prime-members-us |website=aboutamazon.com |publisher=[[Amazon (company)|Amazon]] |access-date=6 May 2026}}</ref>

In March 2026, Amazon announced that 1-hour and 3-hour delivery options were available in some locations, with discounted delivery fees for Prime members.<ref>{{cite news |last1=McLymore |first1=Arriana |title=Amazon launches 1-hour shipping in US cities to challenge Walmart |url=https://www.reuters.com/business/retail-consumer/amazon-launches-1-hour-shipping-us-cities-challenge-walmart-2026-03-17/ |access-date=3 June 2026 |work=[[Reuters]] |date=17 March 2026}}</ref> The following May, Amazon debuted Amazon Now, which has a delivery guarantee of 30 minutes or less on selected items such as some groceries and household sundries; Prime members pay a discounted delivery fee.<ref>{{cite news |last=Perez |first=Sarah |date=12 May 2026 |title= Amazon launches 30-minute delivery across the US |url= https://techcrunch.com/2026/05/12/amazon-expands-30-minute-delivery-across-the-u-s/ |work=[[TechCrunch]]|location= |publisher= |access-date=3 June 2026}}</ref>

==Sub-brands==
=== Prime Access===
Prime Access is a discounted membership for qualifying government assistance recipients and income-verified customers. It includes the same features as Prime.<ref>{{cite web |last=Chen |first=Connie|date=December 18, 2024 |title= Everything you need to know about Prime Access, one of Amazon's discounted Prime membership|url= https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/retail/amazon-prime-access-discounted-membership |website=Amazon |location= |publisher= |access-date=April 2, 2026}}</ref><ref>{{cite news |last= Schoeber|first= Tyler |date=July 28, 2025|title=Amazon Prime for $6.99? Meet Prime Access |url=https://www.tvguide.com/news/what-is-prime-access/|work=[[TV Guide]] |location= |publisher= |access-date=April 2, 2026}}</ref>

=== Prime Music ===
Amazon Music is an ad-free [[music streaming service]] that is included in the cost of the standard Amazon Prime membership. It began in 2007. In November 2022, the music catalog was significantly expanded to 100 million songs. Amazon offers a separate subscription service called Amazon Music Unlimited, which costs $11.99 per month for Prime members and $12.99 per month for non-members.

===Prime Video===
{{main|Amazon Prime Video}}
The service debuted on September 7, 2006, as Amazon Unbox in the United States. On September 4, 2008, the service was renamed Amazon Video on Demand. The Unbox name still refers to the local program, which {{as of|2014|August|lc=y}} is no longer available for downloading purchased instant videos. On February 22, 2011, the service rebranded as Amazon Instant Video.

The services grew and in 2011 Amazon bought UK based streaming and by mail DVD rental service named Lovefilm in 2011 and now the combined services were relaunched as Prime Video.

===Prime Gaming===
Amazon re-branded its Twitch Prime to Amazon Prime Gaming in 2020.<ref>{{cite web|last=Dohare|first=Utkarsh|title=Amazon Prime Gaming: Rebranded but available in India?|url=https://gamzo.in/amazon-prime-gaming-second-attempt-by-amazon/|url-status=live|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200916125302/https://gamzo.in/amazon-prime-gaming-second-attempt-by-amazon/|archive-date=September 16, 2020|access-date=August 16, 2020|website=Gamzo|language=en-US}}</ref><ref name=":15">{{cite web|last1=Peters|first1=Jay|date=August 10, 2020|title=Amazon rebrands Twitch Prime as Prime Gaming|url=https://www.theverge.com/2020/8/10/21358463/amazon-twitch-prime-gaming-rebrand|access-date=August 10, 2020|website=The Verge|language=en|archive-date=August 10, 2020|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200810191518/https://www.theverge.com/2020/8/10/21358463/amazon-twitch-prime-gaming-rebrand|url-status=live}}</ref> Amazon Prime subscribers also get Prime Gaming at no additional cost.<ref>{{cite news|last=Krol|first=Jacob|date=August 13, 2020|title=Prime Gaming: Here's what you need to know|url=https://www.cnn.com/2020/08/10/cnn-underscored/prime-gaming-guide-amazon/index.html|url-status=live|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200916171007/https://www.cnn.com/2020/08/10/cnn-underscored/prime-gaming-guide-amazon/index.html|archive-date=September 16, 2020|access-date=August 16, 2020|website=CNN Underscored|language=en}}</ref> The main difference is that "to access Prime Gaming, customers don't need to have a Twitch account (as they did for Twitch Prime)".<ref>{{Cite web|last=Spangler|first=Todd|date=August 10, 2020|title=Amazon Kills Off Twitch Prime Name, Replaces It With 'Prime Gaming' Perk With Free Games and Content|url=https://variety.com/2020/digital/news/amazon-twitch-prime-gaming-replaced-free-games-1234730422/|url-status=live|access-date=October 7, 2021|website=Variety|language=en-US|archive-date=October 7, 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20211007205557/https://variety.com/2020/digital/news/amazon-twitch-prime-gaming-replaced-free-games-1234730422/}}</ref>

Prime Gaming subscribers can redeem free video games, as well as various rewards in external video games such as digital loot, currency or cosmetics that would typically cost money or are exclusive. The selection of games and rewards on offer changes over time, but redeemed games and rewards can be kept even after one's Prime Gaming subscription ends. Additionally, Prime Gaming allows for a free paid subscription to one [[Twitch (service)|Twitch]] affiliate or partner per month.<ref name=":15" /><ref>{{cite web |last1=Ramirez |first1=Louis |title=15 best Prime member benefits to get the most out of your subscription |url=https://www.tomsguide.com/deals/best-prime-member-benefits |website=Tom's Guide |access-date=16 July 2023 |language=en |date=6 October 2022}}</ref><ref name=":14">{{Cite news|last=Statt|first=Nick|date=August 20, 2018|title=Twitch Prime members will lose ad-free viewing next month|work=The Verge|url=https://www.theverge.com/2018/8/20/17761760/twitch-prime-turbo-ad-free-benefit-end-over-september-14|access-date=August 20, 2018|archive-date=August 20, 2018|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180820213141/https://www.theverge.com/2018/8/20/17761760/twitch-prime-turbo-ad-free-benefit-end-over-september-14|url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web|last=Goslin|first=Austen|date=August 10, 2020|title=Amazon renames Twitch Prime to Prime Gaming|url=https://www.polygon.com/2020/8/10/21361946/amazon-twitch-prime-gaming-new-name-benefits|access-date=October 7, 2021|website=Polygon|language=en-US|archive-date=October 7, 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20211007205556/https://www.polygon.com/2020/8/10/21361946/amazon-twitch-prime-gaming-new-name-benefits|url-status=live}}</ref>

On October 1, 2025, Amazon announced they would be retiring the Prime Gaming brand and rebranding it under [[Amazon Luna]].<ref>{{cite web|title=Prime Gaming is becoming part of Amazon Luna.   |url=https://x.com/TwitchSupport/status/1973441073704735052|access-date=October 1, 2025|website=X|language=en-US}}</ref>

===Prime Reading===
Beginning in October 2016, Prime members in the U.S. receive access to a rotating set of Kindle e-books through Prime Reading, in addition to a selection from its “editors’ picks” and access to Amazon Original Stories – original, short fiction and nonfiction published by Amazon. Prime members also have access to books with Audible narration, and a catalogue of eBooks, audiobooks, comics, and manga.<ref>{{cite web |last= |first= |date= |title= Amazon Prime|url= https://www.amazon.com/gp/help/customer/display.html?nodeId=G6LDPN7YJHYKH2J6|website=[[Amazon (company)|Amazon]] |location= |publisher= |access-date=3 June 2026}}</ref>
<ref>{{Cite news|url=https://www.pcmag.com/news/348480/amazon-bulks-up-prime-with-free-ebooks|title=Amazon Bulks Up Prime With Unlimited 'Reading'|last=Moscaritolo|first=Angela|date=October 5, 2016|work=[[PC Magazine]]|access-date=January 2, 2018|archive-date=January 3, 2018|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180103133757/https://www.pcmag.com/news/348480/amazon-bulks-up-prime-with-free-ebooks|url-status=live}}</ref> Some magazines and travel guides are also available through the service.<ref>{{Cite magazine|url=https://time.com/4519370/amazon-prime-reading/|title=Amazon's Latest Prime Perk Is Great for Readers|last=Fitzpatrick|first=Alex|date=October 5, 2016|magazine=[[Time (magazine)|Time]]|access-date=January 2, 2018|publisher=[[Time Inc.]]|archive-date=January 2, 2018|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180102010556/http://time.com/4519370/amazon-prime-reading/|url-status=live}}</ref> Prime Reading is unrelated to Kindle Unlimited and Kindle First, both of which continue to be available or the Kindle Owners Lending Library, which was discontinued in January 2021.<ref>{{Cite news|url=https://www.usatoday.com/story/tech/news/2016/10/06/amazon-introduces-prime-reading-subscribers/91664356/|title=Amazon introduces Prime Reading for subscribers|last=Molina|first=Brett|date=October 6, 2016|work=[[USA Today]]|access-date=January 2, 2018|archive-date=October 7, 2016|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20161007093118/https://www.usatoday.com/story/tech/news/2016/10/06/amazon-introduces-prime-reading-subscribers/91664356/|url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web|date=January 17, 2021|title=Kindle Owners' Lending Library|url=https://kdp.amazon.com/en_US/help/topic/G201392160?tdr|access-date=November 5, 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210117165210/https://kdp.amazon.com/en_US/help/topic/G201392160?tdr|archive-date=January 17, 2021}}</ref>

===Prime Pantry===
Amazon Prime Pantry was a service of [[Amazon (company)|Amazon]] available only to Prime members that packaged everyday (non-bulk) [[food preservation]] [[Grocery store|grocery]] store items into a single box for delivery for a flat fee. The service was available in the [[United States]], [[Austria]], [[France]], [[Germany]], [[India]],<ref>{{cite web|url=http://www.medianama.com/2017/05/223-amazon-pantry-expansion-2/|title=Amazon Pantry Expansion|website=medianama.com|date=May 2017|access-date=February 15, 2023|archive-date=December 3, 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20221203134725/https://www.medianama.com/2017/05/223-amazon-pantry-expansion-2/|url-status=live}}</ref> [[Italy]], [[Japan]], [[Spain]], and the [[United Kingdom]]. Amazon discontinued the program in different locations on different dates, making the items formerly found exclusively in Prime Pantry available for purchase in the main store. An ever-changing but limited variety of products was offered, but the range actually decreased from when the service was first launched. While selecting items within the Prime Pantry program, each item listed the percentage of space it will take up inside the delivery box. A running total showed how full this box is. The delivery fee remained the same regardless of the filling percentage.<ref>{{cite web|last=Walpole|first=Jennifer|title=Amazon Prime Pantry: Order discounted groceries online|url=http://agbeat.com/business-news/amazon-prime-pantry-order-discounted-groceries-online/|website=The American Genius|date=April 28, 2014|access-date=April 28, 2014|archive-date=June 30, 2015|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150630065542/http://agbeat.com/business-news/amazon-prime-pantry-order-discounted-groceries-online/|url-status=live}}</ref> By the end of 2020 the service had been discontinued in all locations.<ref>{{cite web |author=Vicki Fowler |url=https://tamebay.com/2021/01/amazon-discontinue-prime-pantry-in-the-us.html |title=Amazon discontinue Prime Pantry in the US |publisher=Tamebay |date=January 10, 2021 |accessdate=March 15, 2021 |archive-date=January 25, 2021 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210125173909/https://tamebay.com/2021/01/amazon-discontinue-prime-pantry-in-the-us.html |url-status=live }}</ref>

===Prime Now===
{{main|Prime Now}}

In December 2014, Amazon announced that as a benefit to Prime members located in parts of [[Manhattan]] and [[New York City]] the capability to get products delivered to them within one hour for a fee of $7.99, or within two hours for no additional fee. As of 2014, 25,000 daily essential products were available with this delivery service.<ref>{{cite magazine|url=https://www.wired.com/2014/12/prime-now/|title=Prime Now|magazine=[[Wired (magazine)|Wired]]|access-date=December 22, 2014|archive-date=July 23, 2020|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200723193232/https://www.wired.com/2014/12/prime-now/|url-status=live}}</ref> In February 2015, the service was extended to include all of Manhattan.<ref>{{cite news|url=https://techcrunch.com/2015/02/17/amazon-prime-now-one-hour-delivery-goes-live-across-manhattan/?ncid=rss#8dw0GT:n1G|title=Amazon's One-Hour Delivery Service Goes Live Across Manhattan|access-date=February 17, 2015|archive-date=February 19, 2019|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190219134111/https://techcrunch.com/2015/02/17/amazon-prime-now-one-hour-delivery-goes-live-across-manhattan/?ncid=rss#8dw0GT:n1G|url-status=live}}</ref> By mid-2016, it had been expanded in the United States to include parts of [[Chicago]], [[Miami]], [[Baltimore]],<ref>{{cite news|url=https://www.baltimoresun.com/2015/03/19/amazon-launches-one-hour-delivery-service-in-baltimore/|title=Amazon launches one-hour delivery service in Baltimore|work=[[The Baltimore Sun]]|date=March 19, 2015|access-date=March 19, 2015|first1=Colin|last1=Campbell|first2=Kevin|last2=Rector|first3=Jessica|last3=Anderson|url-status=live|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150319190538/http://www.baltimoresun.com/business/bs-bz-amazon-now-20150319-story.html|archive-date=March 19, 2015}}</ref> [[Seattle]], [[Dallas]], [[Atlanta]], [[Austin, Texas|Austin]], [[Nashville, Tennessee|Nashville]], [[Portland, Oregon|Portland]], [[San Antonio]], and [[Tampa, Florida|Tampa]].<ref>[https://www.amazon.com/b/?node=10481056011] {{Webarchive|url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180301153626/https://primenow.amazon.com/onboard?sourceUrl=%2F|date=March 1, 2018}}. Retrieved March 19, 2015</ref><ref>{{cite web | url=http://www.digitaltrends.com/mobile/amazon-nyc-subway-deliveries/#ixzz3aalLarja | title=Amazon takes the NYC subway to shorten delivery times | work=Digital Trends | date=May 18, 2015 | access-date=May 19, 2015 | author=Andre Revilla | archive-date=July 8, 2020 | ===Prime for Young Adults===
On June 17, 2025, Prime Student relaunched as Prime for Young Adults, a discounted membership for  18-24-year-olds and higher education students. It includes the same features as Prime.<ref>{{cite web |last= |first=|date=August 20, 2025 |title= Score big with Prime for Young Adults—Amazon's discounted Prime membership|url= https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/retail/amazon-prime-for-young-adults |website=Amazon|location= |publisher= |access-date=April 2, 2026}}</ref><ref>{{cite news |last= Tingley|first= Anna|date=June 17, 2025|title=Amazon Announces 'Prime for Young Adults' Membership at Discounted Price Following Six-Month Free Trial= |url=https://variety.com/2025/shopping/news/amazon-prime-for-young-adults-membership-sign-up-online-1236434021/|work=[[Variety (magazine)|Variety]] |location= |publisher= |access-date=April 2, 2026}}</ref>

===Amazon Key===
'''In-Garage'''<br />At [[Consumer Electronics Show|CES 2019]], Amazon announced a partnership with the [[Chamberlain Group]], allowing packages to be placed in customers' garages with myQ-enabled openers as part of the Key service.<ref>{{Cite news|url=https://arstechnica.com/gadgets/2019/01/amazon-attempts-less-creepy-delivery-by-placing-packages-in-your-garage/|title=Amazon attempts less-creepy delivery by placing packages in your garage|last=Palladino|first=Valentina|date=January 8, 2019|work=[[Ars Technica]]|access-date=July 26, 2026}}</ref> The service began a wider rollout to Prime members in 50 U.S. cities in April 2019.<ref>{{cite web|last=Khalid|first=Amrita|title=Amazon Key in-garage deliveries are available for Prime customers|url=https://www.engadget.com/2019-04-23-amazon-key-launches-in-garage-deliveries.html|website=[[Engadget]]|date=April 23, 2019|access-date=July 26, 2026}}</ref> It works with smart garage controllers operated through the myQ (Chamberlain and [[LiftMaster]]), Aladdin Connect ([[Genie Company|Genie]]), and OHD Anywhere ([[Overhead Door Corporation]]) apps, and supports Amazon packages as well as [[Amazon Fresh]] and [[Whole Foods Market]] grocery orders in the United States; customers can optionally link a [[Ring (company)|Ring]] camera to view deliveries.<ref name="aboutamazon-igd">{{cite web|title=Amazon Key In-Garage Delivery: What it is and how it works|url=https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/amazon-prime/what-is-amazon-key-in-garage-delivery|website=About Amazon|publisher=[[Amazon (company)|Amazon]]|access-date=July 26, 2026}}</ref>
  
In-Garage Delivery is selected at checkout after a customer links a compatible garage through the Amazon app's Amazon Key settings. It is offered for a per-order fee, which Amazon waives for orders scheduled through its Amazon Day option that consolidates deliveries onto a single chosen day.<ref name="aboutamazon-igd" /> Amazon states that drivers pass background checks, that access is granted through one-time authentication of the driver, package, and delivery location, and that the garage cannot be reopened once a delivery is complete; customers can block garage access or revert to doorstep delivery at any time.<ref name="aboutamazon-igd" />

{{As of|2026}}, In-Garage Delivery is the only Amazon Key delivery option offered to consumers, following the discontinuation of the In-Home and In-Car services.<ref>{{cite web|title=Key In-Home Deliveries|url=https://amazonforum.com/s/question/0D56Q00009VRxaySAD/key-inhome-deliveries|website=Amazon Customer Service Forum|publisher=[[Amazon (company)|Amazon]]|access-date=July 26, 2026}}</ref>

===Prime Air===
{{main|Amazon Prime Air (drone delivery service)}}

''[[60 Minutes]]'' reported on December 1, 2013, that Amazon Prime Air was a possible future [[Package delivery|delivery]] service expected to be in development for several more years. In concept, the process would use [[unmanned aerial vehicle|drones]] to deliver small packages (less than five pounds) within 30 minutes by flying short distances (10–20&nbsp;km) from local Amazon Fulfillment Centers.<ref name=Mash>{{cite web|last=Strange|first=Adario|title=Amazon Unveils Flying Delivery Drones on '60 Minutes'|url=http://mashable.com/2013/12/01/amazon-unveils-flying-robot-delivery-drones/|work=Mashable|access-date=December 1, 2013|archive-date=May 28, 2020|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200528091328/https://mashable.com/2013/12/01/amazon-unveils-flying-robot-delivery-drones/|url-status=live}}</ref><ref name=AirF>{{cite news|last=Hickey|first=Matt|title=Meet Amazon Prime Air, A Delivery-By-Aerial-Drone Project|url=https://www.forbes.com/sites/matthickey/2013/12/01/meet-amazon-prime-air-amazons-delivery-by-aerial-drone-project/|work=Forbes|access-date=December 1, 2013|archive-date=December 2, 2013|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20131202134102/https://www.forbes.com/sites/matthickey/2013/12/01/meet-amazon-prime-air-amazons-delivery-by-aerial-drone-project/|url-status=live}}</ref> In the United States, the project will require the [[Federal Aviation Administration]] to approve commercial use of unmanned drones.<ref name=TV>{{cite web|last=Pierce|first=David|title=Delivery drones are coming: Jeff Bezos promises half-hour shipping with Amazon Prime Air|url=https://www.theverge.com/2013/12/1/5164340/delivery-drones-are-coming-jeff-bezos-previews-half-hour-shipping|work=The Verge|date=December 2013|publisher=Vox Media|access-date=December 1, 2013|archive-date=December 2, 2013|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20131202052055/https://www.theverge.com/2013/12/1/5164340/delivery-drones-are-coming-jeff-bezos-previews-half-hour-shipping|url-status=live}}</ref>

In July 2014, it was revealed the company was developing its 8th and 9th drone prototypes, some that could fly 50 miles an hour and carry 5-pound packages, and had applied to the FAA to test them.<ref name="FAAamazon">{{cite news|title=E-commerce giant Amazon seeks FAA nod for testing drones|url=http://www.seattlebulletin.com/index.php/sid/223727243/scat/9d7afd9766a94f28/ht/E-commerce-giant-Amazon-seeks-FAA-nod-for-testing-drones|access-date=July 11, 2014|publisher=Seattle Bulletin|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20140714152133/http://www.seattlebulletin.com/index.php/sid/223727243/scat/9d7afd9766a94f28/ht/E-commerce-giant-Amazon-seeks-FAA-nod-for-testing-drones|archive-date=July 14, 2014|url-status=dead}}</ref> The project is not yet in flight as of January 2021, though Amazon did receive FAA approval in the US in August 2020.<ref>{{cite web|last=Palmer|first=Annie|date=August 31, 2020|title=Amazon wins FAA approval for Prime Air drone delivery fleet|url=https://www.cnbc.com/2020/08/31/amazon-prime-now-drone-delivery-fleet-gets-faa-approval.html|access-date=January 30, 2021|website=CNBC|language=en|archive-date=January 29, 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210129091031/https://www.cnbc.com/2020/08/31/amazon-prime-now-drone-delivery-fleet-gets-faa-approval.html|url-status=live}}</ref>

On June 13, 2022, Amazon announced that they will be delivering products using Prime Air drones to customers residing in the small town of Lockeford, California. At the time of announcement, there is no exact launch date other than "later this year", as Amazon awaits permission from the FAA and Lockeford officials.<ref>{{Cite web |date=2022-06-13 |title=Amazon Prime Air prepares for drone deliveries |url=https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/transportation/amazon-prime-air-prepares-for-drone-deliveries |access-date=2022-06-16 |website=US About Amazon |language=en |archive-date=February 14, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230214073109/https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/transportation/amazon-prime-air-prepares-for-drone-deliveries |url-status=live }}</ref>

==Prime Day==
<!-- [[Prime Day]] redirects here. -->
On July 15, 2015, to commemorate the website's 20th anniversary, Amazon held its first '''Prime Day'''. The event is characterized by a number of sales and promotions exclusive to Amazon Prime subscribers, with Amazon initially promoting that it would feature "more deals than [[Black Friday (shopping)|Black Friday]]".<ref>{{cite web|url=https://www.theverge.com/2015/7/6/8899669/amazon-prime-day-bigger-than-black-friday-20th-birthday|title=Amazon says 20th birthday celebration will be bigger than Black Friday|last1=McCormick|first1=Rich|website=The Verge|date=July 6, 2015|access-date=November 21, 2015|archive-date=July 7, 2015|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150707000533/https://www.theverge.com/2015/7/6/8899669/amazon-prime-day-bigger-than-black-friday-20th-birthday|url-status=live}}</ref> The inaugural Prime Day faced criticism over the quality of the discounts offered, with many of them being tied to items not in high demand. Some users jokingly described the event as a "[[Garage sale|yard sale]]", and Walmart also countered the event with a promotional blog post arguing that customers "shouldn't have to pay $100 to find great deals". Amazon defended criticism of the event, stating that order volume on the website had "surpassed" Black Friday sales in 2014.<ref>{{cite web|url=https://money.cnn.com/2015/07/15/news/amazon-walmart-sales/index.html|title=Amazon 'Prime Day' shattered global sales records|last=Garcia|first=Ahiza|date=July 15, 2015|website=CNNMoney|access-date=July 15, 2019|archive-date=July 17, 2015|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150717231743/https://money.cnn.com/2015/07/15/news/amazon-walmart-sales/index.html|url-status=dead}}</ref><ref>{{cite web|url=https://techcrunch.com/2015/07/16/amazon-says-prime-day-was-bigger-than-black-friday-and-will-be-held-again/|title=Amazon Says Prime Day Was Bigger Than Black Friday And Will Be Held Again|website=TechCrunch |date=July 16, 2015 |access-date=July 15, 2019|archive-date=July 15, 2019|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190715170429/https://techcrunch.com/2015/07/16/amazon-says-prime-day-was-bigger-than-black-friday-and-will-be-held-again/|url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{cite web|url=https://techcrunch.com/2015/07/06/amazon-prime-day/|title=Amazon Marks 20th Anniversary With "Prime Day," Its Answer To Black Friday|website=TechCrunch |date=July 6, 2015 |access-date=July 15, Hammond]], and [[James May]], formerly of BBC's ''[[Top Gear (2002 TV series)|Top Gear]]'', to begin working on ''[[The Grand Tour]]'' for Amazon Prime Video, which was released in 2016.

On July 13, 2016, Amazon Prime said customers placed 60 percent more orders worldwide on "Prime Day".<ref>{{cite web|url=https://www.yahoo.com/tech/amazon-says-prime-day-orders-jump-60-percent-141432191--finance.html|title=Amazon Says Prime Day Orders Jump 60 Percent over Last Year|date=July 13, 2016 |access-date=January 6, 2017|archive-date=July 14, preceded by a concert event headlined by [[Ariana Grande]], and streamed on [[Amazon Prime Video|Amazon Video]] and [[Twitch (service)|Twitch]].<ref>{{cite news|url=https://variety.com/2018/biz/news/ariana-grande-to-headline-amazon-music-prime-day-concert-1202869429/|title=Ariana Grande to Headline Amazon Music Prime Day Concert|website=[[Variety (magazine)|Variety]]|date=July 10, 2018|access-date=July 12, 2019|archive-date=July 12, concert was held on July 10 ahead of Prime Day starting on July 15, and streamed exclusively for Prime subscribers, featuring [[Taylor Swift]], [[Dua Lipa]], [[Becky G]], and [[SZA (singer)|SZA]].<ref>{{cite magazine|magazine=Billboard|title=Taylor Swift Sings 'Shake It Off,' 'Blank Space' & More at Amazon Prime Day Concert: In 2018, Prime Day first became tied to protests of Amazon and employee strikes due to [[criticism of Amazon]]. Supporters of these actions have urged [[boycott]]s of Amazon during Prime Day as solidarity, covering all services provided by the company and its subsidiaries.<ref>{{Cite news|url=https://www.theguardian.com/technology/2019/jul/15/prime-day-activists-plan-protests-in-us-cities-and-a-boycott-of-e-commerce-giant|title=Prime Day: activists plan protests in US cities and a boycott of e-commerce giant|last=Paul|first=Kari|date=July 15, 2019|work=The Guardian|access-date=July 15, 2019 |issn=0261-3077|archive-date=July 15, 2019|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190715063452/https://www.theguardian.com/technology/2019/jul/15/prime-day-activists-plan-protests-in-us-cities-and-a-boycott-of-e-commerce-giant|url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{Cite magazine|url=https://www.wired.com/story/strikes-boycotts-and-outages-mar-amazon-prime-day/|title=Strikes, Boycotts, and Outages Mar Amazon Prime Day|last=Tiku|first=Nitasha|date=July 16, 2018|magazine=Wired|access-date=July 15, 2019|issn=1059-1028|archive-date=May 1, web|url=https://fortune.com/2019/07/08/amazon-prime-day-2019-strike/|title=Amazon Warehouse Workers Plan Strike During Prime Day 2019|website=Fortune |access-date=July 15, 2019|archive-date=July 9, In 2020, Prime Day was postponed in the US and Canada due to the [[COVID-19 pandemic]], and was held from October 13–14. Prime Day was held in India on August 6–7.<ref name="Brown 2020">{{cite web | last=Brown | first=Dalvin | title=Amazon Prime Day is Coming to the U.S. This Year, but 'Later than Usual' | website=USA TODAY | date=July 21, 2020 | url=https://www.usatoday.com/story/tech/2020/07/21/amazon-prime-day-delayed-due-pandemic-but-its-still-coming/5477575002/ | access-date=July 21, 2020 | archive-date=July 21, 2020 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200721125104/https://www.usatoday.com/story/tech/2020/07/21/amazon-prime-day-delayed-due-pandemic-but-its-still-coming/5477575002/ | url-status=live }}</ref>

In May 2021, Prime Day was postponed indefinitely in Canada due to COVID-19.<ref>{{Cite web|date=May 7, 2021|title=Amazon postpones Prime Day in Canada due to COVID-19 outbreaks|url=https://www.cbc.ca/news/business/amazon-prime-day-canada-1.6018520|url-status=live|access-date=June 4, 2021|publisher=Canadian Press|archive-date=May 17, 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210517140153/https://www.cbc.ca/news/business/amazon-prime-day-canada-1.6018520}}</ref>

In 2022, building on the delayed October 2020 version, Amazon launched a second annual Prime-exclusive sales event in October, initially called the Prime Early Access Sale, and then renamed '''Prime Big Deal Days''' in 2023.<ref name="wirecutter-pbdd-2023">{{cite web|url=https://www.nytimes.com/wirecutter/money/amazon-prime-big-deal-days-2023/|title=What to Know About Amazon's Second Prime Day, Prime Big Deal Days 2023|first=Kaitlin|last=Mahar|work=[[Wirecutter (website)|Wirecutter]]|publisher=[[The New York Times Company]]|date=September 21, 2023|access-date=October 7, 2024}}</ref> The fall event is also referred to as "Prime Day", or as "October Prime Day", by some publications.<ref name="wirecutter-pbdd-2024">{{cite web|url=https://www.nytimes.com/wirecutter/money/best-early-amazon-prime-big-deal-days-deals-2024-1007/|title=Amazon Prime Big Deal Days Starts Tomorrow. But Here Are the 80+ Best Deals You Can Shop Now.|work=Wirecutter|publisher=The New York Times Company|date=October 7, 2024|access-date=October 7, 2024}}</ref>

In 2023, Prime Day was held from July 11–12, and Prime Big Deal Days was held from October 10–11.<ref>{{Cite web |title=When is Amazon Prime Day 2024 and other key eCommerce dates. |url=https://www.huboo.com/ecommerce/when-is-amazon-prime-day-2024-and-other-key-ecommerce-dates-this-year/ |access-date=2024-07-16 |website=eCommerce Order Fulfilment & Storage |language=en-GB|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20240226080043/https://www.huboo.com/ecommerce/when-is-amazon-prime-day-2024-and-other-key-ecommerce-dates-this-year/|archive-date=2024-02-26}}</ref><ref name="wirecutter-pbdd-2023" />

In 2024, Prime Day was held from July 16–17<ref>{{Cite web |last=Schulz |first=Gabe Hauari and Bailey |title=When is Amazon Prime Day 2024? Dates, deals and what to know about summer sales event |url=https://www.usatoday.com/story/money/shopping/2024/07/15/amazon-prime-day-2024/74382648007/ |access-date=2024-07-16 |website=USA TODAY |language=en-US}}</ref> and was available in the following countries: Australia, Austria, Belgium, Brazil, Canada, Egypt, France, Germany, Italy, Japan, Luxembourg, Mexico, the Netherlands, Poland, Portugal, Saudi Arabia, Singapore, Spain, Sweden, Turkey, the United Arab Emirates, the United States and the United Kingdom.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Amazon Prime Day 2024: How to get deals July 16–17 |url=https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/retail/when-is-prime-day-2024 |access-date=2024-07-16 |website=www.aboutamazon.com |date=June 16, 2022 |language=en}}</ref> The 2024 edition of Prime Big Deal Days was held from October 8–9.<ref name="wirecutter-pbdd-2024" /><ref>{{Cite web |title=Amazon's Prime Big Deal Days 2024 event is October 8–9. Here are 5 tips to save big during the event. |url=https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/retail/amazon-announces-prime-big-deal-days-2024-dates |date=September 17, 2024 |access-date=October 7, 2024 |website=www.aboutamazon.com |language=en}}</ref>

In 2025, Prime Day took place from July 8 to 11, extending to four days instead of  two. Prime Big Deal Days ran from October 7 to 8, 2025.<ref>{{cite news |last1=Palmer |first1=Annie |title=Amazon extends Prime Day to four days, starting July 8 |url=https://www.cnbc.com/2025/06/17/amazon-extends-prime-day-to-four-days-starting-july-8-.html |access-date=3 June 2026 |work=[[CNBC]] |date=17 June 2025}}</ref> In 2026, Prime Day is scheduled to occur June 23–26.<ref>{{cite web |last= |first= |date= |title= When is Amazon Prime Day 2026? Shop deals June 23–26|url= https://www.aboutamazon.com/news/retail/amazon-prime-day-2026-date |website=[[Amazon (company)|Amazon]] |location= |publisher= |access-date=3 June 2026}}</ref>

Prime Day has demonstrated cases of misleading discounting. Various items of 2025's Prime Day shows discounts from a marked up price set months prior as revealed by price tracking website [[Camelcamelcamel]]. Certain items also showed the discounted price to be identical to the prices at other retailers. List price inflation by Amazon Prime has been demonstrated multiple times in previous years, including 2022, 2019, and 2017. Critics have also accused Prime Day as encouraging impulse shopping.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Legum |first=Judd |title=Prime Day is a scam |url=https://popular.info/p/prime-day-is-a-scam |access-date=2025-11-17 |website=popular.info |language=en}}</ref>

==Availability==
{{As of|2025|6|post=,}} Prime memberships are available in 28 countries: Australia, Austria, Belgium, Brazil, Canada, China, Colombia, France, Germany, India, Ireland, Italy, Japan, Luxembourg, Malaysia, Mexico, Netherlands, Poland, Portugal, Saudi&nbsp;Arabia, Singapore, South Africa, Spain, Sweden, Thailand, Turkey, United&nbsp;Kingdom, United&nbsp;States.<ref>{{cite web |url=https://www.emarketer.com/content/amazon-around-the-world |title=Amazon Around the World - Insider Intelligence Trends, Forecasts & Statistics |website=EMarketer.com|first=Jasmine|last=Enberg |date=November 13, 2018|accessdate=March 15, 2021 |archive-date=April 12, 2021 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210412005958/https://www.emarketer.com/content/amazon-around-the-world |url-status=live }}</ref>{{fix|text=new countries not added}}

The service stopped working in Russia in March 2022,<ref>{{cite web | title=Amazon приостановила работу с Россией - сервис Prime Video больше недоступен, отправка товаров прекращена, New World будет снята с продажи | date=March 9, 2022 | url=https://gamemag.ru/news/164886/amazon-russia }}</ref><ref>{{cite web | title=Amazon Prime Video ограничил доступ к фильмам и сериалам для подписчиков из РФ — Жизнь на DTF | date=March 9, 2022 | url=https://dtf.ru/life/1111985-amazon-prime-video-ogranichil-dostup-k-filmam-i-serialam-dlya-podpischikov-iz-rf }}</ref> and in Belarus in January 2023.<ref>{{Cite web | url=https://1prof.by/news/stil-zhizni/strimingovyj-servis-prime-video-ot-amazon-otmenil-vse-belorusskie-podpiski/ | title=Стриминговый сервис prime video от amazon отменил все белорусские подписки | language=ru | trans-title=Amazon Prime Video, a streaming service, has cancelled all Belarusian subscriptions | website=1prof.by}}</ref>

{{As of|2025|post=,}} while a valid debit or credit card is required to be provided as a backup payment method, it is possible to pay for Amazon Prime subscription fees with Amazon gift card balance in the following countries:<ref>{{cite web |url=https://milestomemories.com/use-amazon-gift-cards-for-prime-membership/ |title= You Can Now Use Amazon Gift Cards for Prime Membership and Video Subscriptions|date= December 18, 2020}}</ref> Japan,<ref>{{Cite web|url=https://www.amazon.co.jp/-/en/gp/help/customer/display.html?nodeId=201910200|title=The Amazon Prime Membership Fee - Amazon Customer Service}}</ref> Italy,<ref>{{cite web |url=https://www.amazon.it/b?ie=UTF8&node=56922885031 |title=Amazon.it: Buoni Regalo Prime: Buoni regalo |website=Amazon.it }}</ref> Spain,<ref>{{cite web |url=https://www.amazon.es/b?ie=UTF8&node=56935322031 |title=Amazon.es: Tarjetas Regalo Amazon Prime: Cheques regalo |website=Amazon.es }}</ref> Portugal (shared site domain with Spain), Mexico,<ref name="prime-mx-faq-page">{{Cite web|url=https://www.amazon.com.mx/b?ie=UTF8&node=23483337011|title=Amazon.com.mx}}</ref> Canada, France,<ref>{{cite web |url=https://www.amazon.fr/b?ie=UTF8&node=56187355031 |title=Amazon.fr : Cartes Cadeaux Prime : Boutique cartes cadeaux |website=Amazon.fr }}</ref> the United Kingdom<ref>{{cite web |url=https://www.amazon.co.uk/b?node=56336462031 |title=Amazon.co.uk: Prime Gift Cards: Gift Cards |website=Amazon UK }}</ref> the United States, Netherlands (since November 2023),<ref>{{Cite web|url=https://www.primevideo.com/help?nodeId=TtmpPpt4SHTjHQsAEX|title=Prime Video: Discover Amazon Prime Video Payment Options}}</ref> Saudi Arabia (since November 2023),<ref name="prime-sa-faq-page">{{Cite web|url=https://www.amazon.sa/-/en/b?ie=UTF8&node=27155493031|title=- All Categories: Buy Online at Best Prices in Saudi &#124; Souq is now Amazon.sa}}</ref> Germany (since November 2023), Luxembourg (shared site domain with Germany).

In Saudi Arabia<ref name="prime-sa-faq-page" /> and Mexico,<ref name="prime-mx-faq-page" /> users can pay for a Prime membership using only Amazon Gift Cards, without having to provide a credit or debit card beforehand. A free trial is not provided if there is no credit or debit card added to a user's account.

The ability to pay for Amazon Prime with Amazon gift card balance was removed in India in late 2024/2025.

==Competition==
The success of Prime has spurred [[brick-and-mortar]] businesses to launch similar paid subscription programs, with [[Walmart]] with its [[Walmart+]] program being the most notable example.<ref>{{cite web | title=Amazon Prime vs. Walmart+: What you need to know about the rival services | website=[[CNBC]] | date=December 4, 2020 | url=https://www.cnbc.com/select/amazon-prime-vs-walmart-plus/ }}</ref> [[Target Corporation|Target]] (via Target Circle 360)<ref>{{cite web | title=Save 50% on Target's paid membership program with this limited-time deal | date=March 17, 2025 | url=https://www.today.com/shop/what-is-target-circle-360-rcna142018 }}</ref> and [[Kroger]] (via Kroger Boost, as well as other Boost programs branded with Kroger's various brands)<ref>{{cite web | title=7 Smart Shopper: How Kroger's Boost Membership can help you save on groceries | date=July 17, 2025 | url=https://www.wxyz.com/news/smart-shopper/7-smart-shopper-how-krogers-boost-membership-can-help-you-save-on-groceries }}</ref> also have paid subscription programs similar to Prime.

'''The Descartes Systems Group Inc.''' (commonly referred to as '''Descartes''' or '''DSG''') is a Canadian [[Multinational corporation|multinational]] [[technology company]] specializing in [[logistics]] software, [[supply chain management software]], and [[Cloud computing|cloud]]-based services for logistics businesses.

Descartes is known as one of the earliest logistics technology companies to adopt an on-demand [[business model]] and sell its [[software as a service]] (SaaS) via the Internet. In addition, the company operates the Global Logistics Network, an extensive electronic messaging system used by [[freight companies]], manufacturers, [[Distribution (business)|distributors]], retailers, customs brokers, government agencies, and other interested parties to exchange logistics and [[customs]] information.

Headquartered in [[Waterloo, Ontario|Waterloo]], [[Ontario]], Canada, Descartes is a [[publicly traded company]] with shares listed on the [[Nasdaq Stock Market|NASDAQ Stock Market]] (NASDAQ: DSGX) and [[Toronto Stock Exchange]] (TSX: DSG). It has offices in the Americas, Europe, the [[Middle East]], Africa, and the [[Asia-Pacific|Asia Pacific region]].

== History ==
Descartes was founded in 1981.<ref>{{Cite web|url=http://markets.businessinsider.com/stock/descartes_systems_group/company-profile|title=Descartes Systems Group Inc. - Profile|website=Business Insider|access-date=March 9, 2017|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20170312041238/http://markets.businessinsider.com/stock/descartes_systems_group/company-profile|archive-date=March 12, 2017|url-status=dead}}</ref>

In 1998, the company made an [[initial public offering]] on the Toronto Stock Exchange,<ref name=":0">{{Cite news|url=https://www.theglobeandmail.com/report-on-business/descartes-executives-to-depart/article20449197/|title=Descartes executives to depart|date=April 22, 2009|work=The Globe and Mail|access-date=July 29, 2016}}</ref> where its common shares traded under the [[stock symbol]] DSG.<ref>{{Cite web|url=http://web.tmxmoney.com/company.php?qm_symbol=DSG|title=Descartes Systems Group Inc. (The)|website=TMX Money|access-date=August 21, 2016}}</ref> Descartes was first listed on the NASDAQ Stock Market in 1999,<ref name=":0" /> with common shares trading under the symbol DSGX.<ref>{{Cite web|url=https://www.nasdaq.com/symbol/dsgx|title=Descartes Systems Group Inc. (The) Common Stock Quote & Summary Data|website=Nasdaq|access-date=August 21, 2016}}</ref> Descartes’ share price peaked during the [[dot-com bubble]] in 2000, and then fell precipitously in the subsequent crash.<ref name=":0" /><ref name=":1">{{Cite news|url=https://www.theglobeandmail.com/report-on-business/ceos-bold-prescription-for-profit-turnaround/article4284715/|title=CEO's bold prescription for profit turnaround|last=Pitts|first=Gordon|date=July 19, 2009|work=The Globe and Mail|access-date=July 26, 2016}}</ref>

In 2001, Descartes switched its business model from selling full-featured [[enterprise software]] licenses to providing on-demand software on a subscription basis, becoming one of the first SaaS providers in the logistics sector.<ref name=":8">{{Cite web|url=http://www.joc.com/economy-watch/descartes-expansion-strategy_20070902.html|title=Descartes' expansion strategy|last=Field|first=Alan|date=September 2, 2007|website=The Journal of Commerce|access-date=July 28, 2016}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web|url=http://www.joc.com/still-standing_20030406.html|title=Still standing|last=Saccomano|first=Ann|date=April 6, 2003|website=The Journal of Commerce|access-date=October 20, 2016}}</ref> After years of losses, Descartes came close to bankruptcy in 2004,<ref name=":1" /> prompting it to aggressively restructure.<ref>{{Cite web|url=http://www.joc.com/economy-watch/descartes-system-crash_20040523.html|title=Descartes' System Crash|date=May 23, 2004|website=Traffic World/The Journal of Commerce|access-date=July 28, 2016}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news|url=https://www.theglobeandmail.com/technology/descartes-profit-exceeds-expectations/article1127774/|title=Descartes profit exceeds expectations|date=December 2, 2005|work=The Globe and Mail|access-date=July 29, 2016}}</ref> The company cut 35% of its workforce<ref>{{Cite news|url=https://www.theglobeandmail.com/technology/descartes-improves-but-year-end-results-weak/article20420041/|title=Descartes improves, but year-end results weak|last=Paddon|first=David|date=March 4, 2005|work=The Globe and Mail|access-date=July 29, 2016}}</ref> and initiated a sweeping transformation of its [[corporate culture]] under CEO Arthur Mesher, who was appointed in 2005.<ref name=":1" /><ref name=":6">{{Cite news|url=https://business.financialpost.com/corporate-culture-awards/descartes-attributes-success-to-humble-servant-culture|title=Descartes attributes success to 'humble servant culture'|date=February 4, 2013|work=Financial Post|access-date=July 25, 2016}}</ref><ref name=":7">{{Cite web|url=https://www.cantechletter.com/2013/11/art-mesher-one-canadas-dynamic-ceos-leaves-descartes1127/|title=Art Mesher, one of Canada's most dynamic CEOs, leaves Descartes|last=Waddell|first=Nick|date=November 27, 2013|website=[[Cantech Letter]]|publisher=Cantech Communications|access-date=July 26, 2016}}</ref>

The company returned to profitability in 2005,<ref>{{Cite news|url=https://www.theglobeandmail.com/report-on-business/descartes-posts-record-profit-after-major-restructuring/article18227832/|title=Descartes posts record profit after major restructuring|date=May 26, 2005|work=The Globe and Mail|access-date=July 29, 2016}}</ref> with Difference Capital Managing Director, Tom Liston, describing this as "one of the most dramatic turnarounds in Canadian corporate history."<ref name=":7" />

In December 2013, Descartes was added to the [[S&P/TSX Composite Index]], an index of the stock (equity) prices of the largest companies on the [[Toronto Stock Exchange]].<ref>{{Cite news|url=http://www.therecord.com/news-story/4273819-descartes-to-join-s-p-tsx-composite-index/|title=Descartes to join S&P/TSX composite index|date=December 16, 2013|work=Waterloo Region Record|access-date=July 27, 2016}}</ref>

By January, 2015, Descartes had posted 41 straight profitable quarters<ref>{{Cite news|url=http://www.therecord.com/news-story/5461025-descartes-posts-41st-consecutive-profitable-quarter/|title=Descartes posts 41st consecutive profitable quarter|date=March 5, 2015|work=Waterloo Region Record|access-date=July 27, 2016}}</ref> and was supplying logistics software and services to more than 10,000 logistics-centric businesses, such as [[ground transportation]] companies, airlines, ocean carriers, [[freight forwarder]]s, manufacturers, distributors, and retailers.<ref>{{Cite news|url=http://www.therecord.com/news-story/2625294-descartes-systems-thrives-in-perfect-storm-/|title=Descartes Systems thrives in 'perfect storm'|date=March 7, 2013|work=Waterloo Region Record|access-date=July 27, 2016}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web|url=https://www.clresearch.com/research/detail.cfm?guid=2C5FD36D-3048-79ED-996A-8C1045ADE570|title=Highlights from Descartes Evolution 2016|last=Grackin|first=Ann|date=April 21, 2016|website=ChainLink Research|access-date=July 28, 2016}}</ref><ref name=":3">{{Cite web|url=http://www.joc.com/international-logistics/logistics-technology/new-delivery-methods-lower-tms-costs-barriers_20130724.html|title=New Delivery Methods Lower TMS Costs, Barriers|last=Biederman|first=David|date=July 24, 2013|website=The Journal of Commerce|access-date=July 28, 2016}}</ref><ref name=":4">{{Cite web|url=http://www.joc.com/trucking-logistics/trucking-through-cloud_20110404.html|title=Trucking Through the Cloud|last=Biederman|first=David|date=April 4, 2011|website=The Journal of Commerce|access-date=July 28, 2016}}</ref> Its customers included [[American Airlines]], [[Delta Air Lines]], [[Air Canada]], [[British Airways]], [[Maersk Group]], [[Hapag-Lloyd]], [[Con-way]], [[Kuehne + Nagel]], [[DHL]], [[The Home Depot]], [[Sears Holdings|Sears Brands]], [[Hallmark Cards]], [[Hasbro]], [[Volvo]], [[Ferrellgas]], [[Del Monte Foods|Del Monte]], [[Toyota]], and [[The Coca-Cola Company]].

In 2018, [[Gartner]] ranked Descartes 6th in its list of the ''Top 20 Supply Chain Management Software Suppliers'', based on revenue of $221 million.<ref>{{Cite web|url=https://www.mmh.com/article/2018_top_20_supply_chain_software_suppliers|title=2018 Top 20 supply chain software suppliers|last=Bond|first=Josh|date=December 10, 2018|website=Modern Materials Handling|access-date=December 13, 2018}}</ref>

In 2025, Descartes was also recognised by the ARC Advisory Group as the leading provider of cloud-based Transport Management Systems (TMS) and the leading provider of fleet management applications.<ref>{{Cite news|url=https://www.arcweb.com/market-analysis/transportation-management-systems|title=Transportation Management Systems|author=ARC Advisory Group|work=ARC Advisory Group|access-date=August 28, 2025}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news|url=https://www.quiverquant.com/news/Descartes+Systems+Group+Recognized+as+Leading+Provider+in+Transportation+Management+Systems+Market+by+ARC+Advisory+Group|title=Descartes Systems Group Recognized as Leading Provider in Transportation Management Systems Market by ARC Advisory Group|date=August 14, 2025|author=Quantitative Quiver|work=Quiver Quantitative|access-date=August 28, 2025}}</ref>

As of 2025, 5 Descartes products are recognised in the Top 50 Supply Chain & Logistics Products list on G2, and the company is recognised as the #1 Grid™ Leader in 12 categories.<ref>{{Cite news|url=https://www.g2.com/best-software-companies/top-supply-chain-and-logistics|title=Best Supply Chain & Logistics Products|author=G2.ai|work=G2.ai|access-date=August 28, 2025}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news|url=https://www.g2.com/sellers/descartes-systems-group|title=Descartes Systems Group|author=G2.ai|work=G2.ai|access-date=August 28, 2025}}</ref>

== Acquisitions ==
Acquisitions have played a key role in Descartes’ growth.<ref name=":5">{{Cite news|url=http://www.therecord.com/news-story/4604857-descartes-plans-big-share-offering/|title=Descartes plans big share offering|last=Pender|first=Terry|date=June 27, 2014|work=Waterloo Region Record|access-date=July 27, 2016}}</ref><ref name=":8" /><ref>{{Cite news|url=http://www.therecord.com/news-story/2579447-descartes-keeping-eye-out-for-acquisition-opportunities/|title=Descartes keeping eye out for acquisition opportunities|date=June 2, 2011|work=Waterloo Region Record|access-date=July 27, 2016}}</ref><ref name=":12">{{Cite news|url=https://business.financialpost.com/investing/trading-desk/descartes-systems-group-inc-upgraded-as-big-acquisition-may-be-coming?__lsa=8500-1c59|title=Descartes Systems Group Inc upgraded as big acquisition may be coming|last=Ratner|first=Jonathan|date=October 19, 2016|work=Financial Post|access-date=October 20, 2016}}</ref> By acquiring niche technology companies, Descartes has expanded its line of logistics software and services, enlarged its customer base, and extended its business geographically.<ref>{{Cite news|url=https://www.wsj.com/articles/sap-links-with-descartes-for-transportation-management-service-1438199806|title=Software Firm Descartes Raising Funds for More Acquisitions|last=Chao|first=Loretta|date=May 26, 2016|work=The Wall Street Journal|access-date=July 28, 2016}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news|url=https://www.wsj.com/articles/todays-top-supply-chain-and-logistics-news-from-wsj-1464345382|title=Today's Top Supply Chain and Logistics News From WSJ|last=Baskin|first=Brian|date=May 27, 2016|work=The Wall Street Journal|access-date=July 28, 2016}}</ref>

== Services and solutions ==
Descartes has expanded into various solution markets and sectors through acquisitions and product development.<ref>{{Cite web |date=December 22, 2025 |title=Descartes Systems Group: A Tale Of Portfolio Expansion Driven by Disciplined Acquisitions and Network Leverage! |url=https://www.smartkarma.com/insights/descartes-systems-group-a-tale-of-portfolio-expansion-driven-by-disciplined-acquisitions-and-network-leverage |access-date=December 24, 2025 |website=Smart Karma}}</ref>

=== Transportation Management ===
Descartes’ Transportation Management solution offers a platform that shippers, freight brokers, and 3PLs can use to synchronize transportation processes and optimize the flow of goods with multimodal TMS, real-time visibility, dock scheduling, and carrier connectivity solutions.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Best Transportation Management Systems Reviews 2025 {{!}} Gartner Peer Insights |url=https://www.gartner.com/reviews/market/transportation-management-systems |access-date=2025-12-24 |website=www.gartner.com |language=en}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |title=The secret of top performers: Transportation management is a competitive weapon {{!}} Supply Chain Dive |url=https://www.supplychaindive.com/news/transportation-management-competition-Descartes/511695/ |access-date=2025-12-24 |website=www.supplychaindive.com |language=en-US}}</ref>

=== Routing, Mobile and Telematics Software ===
[[Fleet management|Fleet Management]] is designed to enhance fleet productivity, optimize performance management, ensure safety, and elevate service levels. Descartes's solutions include Fleet Mobile Applications, Customer Engagement Software, Route Planning, Optimization & Dispatch.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Campbell |first=Tim |title=Descartes' route planning system 'pays for itself after six months', says Ontex |url=https://www.fleetnews.co.uk/news/latest-news/2018/06/01/descartes-route-planning-system-pays-for-itself-after-six-months-says-ontex |access-date=2025-12-24 |website=www.fleetnews.co.uk |language=en}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |title=Descartes Routing, Mobile and Telematics reviews 2025 |url=https://us.fitgap.com/products/020903/descartes-routing-mobile-and-telematics |access-date=2025-12-24 |website=FitGap |language=en}}</ref>

=== Global Trade Intelligence ===
Descartes also provides trade intelligence solutions for compliance, enablement, and analytics. These include tools for managing export controls, sanctions, import classifications, tariffs, [[Free trade agreement|Free Trade Agreements]] (FTAs), and global trade data.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Unlock global trade intelligence to stay competitive in a complex world – Trade for prosperity |url=https://www.tradeforprosperity.co.uk/unlock-global-trade-intelligence-to-stay-competitive-in-a-complex-world/ |access-date=2025-12-24 |language=en-GB}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |title=Descartes showcases global trade intelligence technology innovations |url=https://www.ajot.com/news/descartes-showcases-global-trade-intelligence-technology-innovations |access-date=2025-12-24 |website=www.ajot.com |language=en}}</ref>

=== Customs and Regulatory Compliance ===
Descartes’ Customs and Regulatory Compliance solution supports [[Border trade|cross-border trade]] with preparation, filing, and visibility solutions for cargo security, customs declarations, and other regulatory mandates through international compliance technology. Some of these solutions include customs declarations, export compliance, and [[Foreign-trade zones of the United States|Foreign Trade Zone]] (FTZ) management.<ref>{{Cite web |last=veera |title=How Descartes Customs Integration Improves Compliance |url=https://www.carguber.com/blogs/what-is-the-descartes-customs-system-and-how-does-it-simplify-customs-and-logistics-operations |access-date=2025-12-24 |website=www.carguber.com |language=en-US}}</ref>

=== E-Commerce Shipping & Fulfilment Software ===
Descartes offers e-commerce and shipping software that supports D2C/B2C brands, marketplace sellers, 3PLs, and more with unified tools for inventory, orders, warehouse, and shipping management.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Chain |first=IT Supply |date=2022-03-22 |title=Descartes Combines Warehousing & Shipping Solutions for Ecommerce Companies |url=https://itsupplychain.com/descartes-combines-warehousing-shipping-solutions-for-ecommerce-companies/ |access-date=2025-12-24 |website=IT Supply Chain |language=en-US}}</ref>

=== Broker & Forwarder Enterprise Systems ===
Their Broker & Forwarder Enterprise Systems streamline and operate shipment management, customs compliance, accounting, and [[customer relationship management]]. This solution includes a shipment portal, customs compliance, Foreign Trade Zone (FTZ) management, and Digital Rate Management.<ref>{{Cite web |last=success |first=Your trusted source for investing |title=Descartes' Study Finds 67% of Freight Forwarders and Customs Brokers View Technology as Fundamental to Growth {{!}} INN |url=https://investingnews.com/descartes-study-finds-67-of-freight-forwarders-and-customs-brokers-view-technology-as-fundamental-to-growth/ |access-date=2025-12-24 |website=investingnews.com |language=en}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |title=Descartes Systems Group {{!}} Globenewswire |url=https://via.ritzau.dk/pressemeddelelse/3453520/descartes-systems-group?publisherId=90446 |access-date=2025-12-24 |website=via.ritzau.dk |language=da}}</ref>

=== B2B Connectivity and Messaging ===
Its B2B connectivity and EDI messaging solutions connect hundreds of thousands of parties through the Descartes Global Network, enabling the exchange and transformation of business-critical data. The platform supports a range of electronic document-sharing methods, including EDI, advanced messaging systems, and web-based portal solutions.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Admin |title=Descartes |url=https://scw-mag.com/news/184-descartes/ |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250521190017/https://scw-mag.com/news/184-descartes/ |archive-date=2025-05-21 |access-date=2025-12-24 |website=scw-mag.com |language=en-GB}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=ISM |date=2025-11-13 |title=Optimize Logistics with Supply Chain Solutions |url=https://www.ism.ws/supply-chain/supply-chain-solutions/ |access-date=2025-12-24 |language=en-US}}</ref>

=== Global Logistics Network ===
Descartes’ cloud-based logistics messaging system, the Global Logistics Network (GLN), connects more than 26,000 customers and 200,000 connected parties in more than 160 countries,<ref>{{Cite news|url=https://www.morningstar.com/company-reports/1205508-with-its-neutral-network-descartes-makes-the-global-logistics-market-run-smoothly|title=With Its Neutral Network, Descartes Makes the Global Logistics Market Run Smoothly|date=February 6, 2024|last=Romanoff|first=Dan|work=Morning Star|access-date=December 2, 2025}}</ref> making it one of the world's largest logistics networks. Each year, the GLN carries more than 24 billion messages and manages more than 1 billion shipping routes.

Companies use the network to oversee [[shipping]] orders, file customs paperwork, comply with [[security]] regulations, share information across international supply chains, and automate logistics processes.<ref>{{Cite news|url=https://www.scm.dk/cdm-software-adopts-descartes-global-logistics-networktm-offer-comprehensive-air-cargo-connectivity|title=
Descartes Systems Group|work=IFS|access-date=December 2, 2025}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news|url=https://www.ifs.com/partners/find-an-ifs-partner/descartes-systems-group|title=
CDM Software Adopts Descartes' Global Logistics Network(TM) to Offer Comprehensive Air Cargo Connectivity and Messaging Services|work=SCM.dk|access-date=December 2, 2025}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news|url=https://www.nbcnews.com/id/wbna39398746|title=
Descartes' Federated Network "The GLN" Hits Milestone of 35,000 Connected Trading Partners|work=NBC News|access-date=December 2, 2025}}</ref> In 2015, Descartes signed a deal with German [[business software]] giant [[SAP SE]] that allows users of SAP's transportation management software to access the GLN.<ref>{{Cite news|url=https://www.dcvelocity.com/articles/29350-descartes-integrates-customs-declaration-filing-tool-with-sap-tms|title=
Descartes integrates customs declaration filing tool with SAP TMS|work=DCVelocity|access-date=December 2, 2025}}</ref>
